## Balances (descriptive; vs trailing norms and code expectations, never a consensus)

Norm = the same week of the prior five years (EIA convention, current year excluded). Reading a balance as tight or loose is inference; inventories mostly reflect precautionary demand and supply smoothing, not flow demand. Range position: 0 = the 5-year same-week low, 100 = the high; below 0 or above 100 = outside the range.

- Cushing crude: weak or unstable seasonality (month explains 7% of weekly changes; stability 0.24 between the two halves of 2016-10-28..2026-10-16), so the same-week norm is shaky; vs its trailing 52-week mean: +15.4%. No range alert.
- US jet fuel: weak or unstable seasonality (month explains 7% of weekly changes; stability 0.57 between the two halves of 2016-10-28..2026-10-16), so the same-week norm is shaky; vs its trailing 52-week mean: -3.7%. No range alert.
- US total ex SPR: weak or unstable seasonality (month explains 7% of weekly changes; stability 0.47 between the two halves of 2016-10-28..2026-10-16), so the same-week norm is shaky; vs its trailing 52-week mean: -2.9%. No range alert.

USDA's average miss = the mean gap between this month's projection and the final, from USDA's reliability table in the same file. Stocks-to-use is a consistency check, not a price model: the curve shifts by regime.

Reading convention (inference, not measured): OpenRouter tokens per day (7-day mean): more tokens routed = more model use by developers and agents (one router, not the market); Ramp AI Index: US businesses paying for AI: wider paid adoption; Ramp AI Index: AI share of business card and bill spend: deeper spend per adopter; Ramp AI Index: businesses paying Anthropic: vendor share, not total demand; Ramp AI Index: businesses paying OpenAI: vendor share, not total demand; H100 rental, on demand (median of providers): higher = rented compute tighter (demand ahead of supply); A100 rental, on demand (old generation): old chips holding price = demand absorbing all vintages; Akash GPU utilisation: higher = fuller marketplace (small market: colour only); US data centre construction spending (SAAR): more building put in place; US private electric power construction (SAAR): power build-out; data centres are one of several drivers; TSMC monthly revenue: more leading-edge chips shipped (AI and phones; NT$-sensitive); TSMC monthly revenue, y/y as published: growth rate; Korea chip exports, 1-20 day print: memory and AI-server chip shipments (prev = last month's 1-20); Korea chip exports y/y, 1-20 day print: growth rate (working days differ: see the release); Korea chip exports y/y, monthly final: growth rate; DDR5 16Gb spot (DRAMeXchange, session average): higher = memory tighter (AI servers pull capacity to HBM/DDR5).

Dates: GPU rentals are the week starting that Monday (latest week partial); Akash and DRAMeXchange are the day of the reading; Ramp, Census and TSMC the reference month (published later); Korea Customs the release day. GetDeploying and OpenRouter data are CC BY 4.0; Korea Customs KOGL type 1 (attribution); DRAMeXchange is personal use only (never in a public post).

Not wired (see docs/research/ai-demand-signals.md): Silicon Data H100/H200/B200 rental indices: paid API and portal; public pages show no daily readings; terms bar copying; Taiwan export releases (MOF): the statistics portal timed out from this PC on 21 Oct 2026; TSMC revenue stands in.

## Risk and crowding (descriptive; in paper R)

Risk model: daily log returns of front-month closes (Yahoo continuous contracts, roll gaps not adjusted) spliced with spot/index proxies where a contract has no close (flagged). Current vol = EWMA 0.94; calm correlation = last 250 days; stress correlation = the top-20% turbulence days of the whole history; both shrunk toward constant correlation (Ledoit & Wolf).
Model window 2003-12-02 to 2026-10-26; 1122 stress days; shrinkage calm 0.07, stress 0.02.

**Market state:** turbulence percentile 93 of the last 756 days (2026-10-26); 2 of the last 5 days in the top 20%. HY OAS 3.38% (2026-10-23), +58bp over 21 observations ([FRED](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/BAMLH0A0HYM2)). VIX 17.8, percentile 52 since 1990-01-02 (2026-10-26). Funding **stressing**.

**Book:** 2 ideas, 1.8 effective independent ideas (stress matrix). Daily sd 0.27R calm, 0.72R stress. Open -0.08R; -1.42R more if every stop is hit. Book -1.29R from its 10-day high; -1.29R over 60 days (closed R + open marks; sizing halves at -4.0R).

| Idea | Dir | Size | Last (date) | R now | Stop (daily sd) | 1-sd day (R) | Share of stress risk | Cluster | CFTC pct on side (w/w z; ex-trend pct; report date) | Flags |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| S&P 500 `20261020-1900-s-p-500-long` | long | 1.00 | 7732.25 (2026-10-26) | -0.08 | 7.2 | 0.13 | 33% | 1 | n/a | trend leg flips by roll-off within 5 days (21d, 63d) |
| Copper COMEX vs LME `20261023-2200-copper-comex-vs-lme-relative` | relative | 0.50 | 100.00 (2026-10-23) | +0.00 | 2.7 | 0.19 | 67% | 2 | n/a | - |

Share of stress risk sums to 100%; a negative share is a hedge. CFTC levels mostly restate past returns (returns lead positions in 10 of 10 markets: Sanders, Irwin & Merrin). Crowding is context; it turns red only when funding is stressing. No crowding or CTA flag alerts unless the research loop (agent 12) rates it supported; its measured verdicts are listed under the CTA table.

**Worst historical windows for today's book** (searched 1986-01-03 to 2026-10-26, 10310 days; FRTB-style: the window in which this book would have lost most; spot/index proxies where a contract had no close; markets with no series that day inferred from those that printed (27% of market-days)).

**What hurts this book most** (reverse stress: average moves on its worst 103 days since 1986-01-03, x 0.85 for fat tails; Glasserman, Kang & Kang): ES=F -2.7%, rv:20261023-2200-copper-comex-vs-lme-relative -3.0% = -1.25R a day. Worst days: 2025-07-31, 1987-10-19, 2009-01-14.

**Episode library** (replayed day by day on today's book; relative moves). Stress P&L in R from today's marks. Replays run day by day: 'stops honoured' exits at the first close beyond the stop (a gap can cost more than 1R); 'gapped' holds to the end. Instantaneous shocks cap honoured losses at the stop. k = plausibility: the scenario's Mahalanobis distance under the stress matrix (scaled to its length), converted to a one-market sd equivalent, so 3 is about as rare as a 3-sd move in one market. Stress tests are not early warnings (Borio et al.).

Vol-scaled = each market's move x (its vol now / its vol when the episode began) (Hull & White). Worst inside = the book's lowest point during the window, gapped. Proxy share = share of the loss resting on proxy, screened or inferred days.

**Named driver shocks** (one month; other markets move by their conditional mean given the drivers). Gapped R under the stress matrix and under each crisis window's correlations; isolated = drivers only. A sign flip means the answer depends on which correlations hold.

**CTA levels** (trend-follower proxy, observed closes). Prices at which a simple 1/3/12-month trend model would flip sign: describes likely systematic positioning, not a forecast. Published evidence finds trend-flow price pressure small (about 0.2% a week) and fading or reversing within about 4 weeks (Kang, Rouwenhorst & Tang).
